The Parisi-Sourlas Uplift and Infinitely Many Solvable 4 ​ d \mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028 CFTs


Abstract
Parisi-Sourlas (PS) supersymmetry is known to emerge in some models with random field type of disorder. When PS SUSY is present the d \mathchar 29028 -dimensional theory allows for a d − 2 \mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722 -dimensional description. In this paper we investigate the reversed question and we provide new indications that any given CFT d-2 can be uplifted to a PS SUSY CFT d . We show that any scalar four-point function of a CFT d-2 is mapped to a set of 43 four-point functions of the uplifted CFT d which are related to each other by SUSY and satisfy all necessary bootstrap axioms. As a byproduct we find 43 non trivial relations between conformal blocks across dimensions. We test the uplift in generalized free field theory (GFF) and find that PS SUSY is a powerful tool to bootstrap an infinite class of previously unknown GFF observables. Some of this power is shown to persist in perturbation theory around GFF. We explain why all diagonal minimal models admit an uplift and we show exact results for correlators and CFT data of the 4 ​ d \mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028 uplift of the Ising model. Despite being strongly coupled 4 ​ d \mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028 CFTs, the uplifted minimal models contain infinitely many conserved currents and are expected to be integrable.



Emilio Trevisani

Laboratoire de Physique Théorique et Hautes Énergies,
CNRS
& Sorbonne Université,

4 Place Jussieu, 75252 Paris, France

91191, Gif-sur-Yvette, France

1
Introduction

Parisi-Sourlas supersymmetry was first introduced in
[
1
]
in order to explain a very peculiar behaviour of critical theories with quenched random field type of disorder. The story started a few years prior when Aharony, Imry, and Ma conjectured that the IR fixed point of random field models is described by pure models in two less dimensions
[
2
]
. This was motivated by the behaviour of the Feynman diagrams of the random field Ising model in
\mathchar 29028
dimensions, which in the IR limit match the diagrammatic computations of the pure Ising model in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
dimensions.
Parisi and Sourlas provided an explanation of such behaviour by conjecturing that random field models have emergent supersymmetry in the IR, which they further showed (through a perturbative argument) to be responsible for the dimensional reduction
[
1
]
.
Namely, following picture
1
, the relation
A
of Aharony, Imry, and Ma, was explained by Parisi and Sourlas by a combination of
B
and
C
.

d
f.p.

:

\mathchar 29028
dimensions (RF

d
(PS CFT
d
) and a pure CFT in two lower dimensions (CFT
d-2
).

The explanation by Parisi and Sourlas is very appealing since the emergence of symmetries at a fixed point is not uncommon. Moreover the original perturbative argument for
C
was later made non-perturbative and more rigorous
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
]
. A CFT argument was also recently provided in
[
7
]
.

Unfortunately the beautiful conjecture by Parisi-Sourlas was quickly found to have flaws. In particular the arrow
A
for the Ising model clearly cannot be fully correct. Indeed the RF Ising model has a critical point in
\mathchar 28723\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28725

[
8
]
, while the pure
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12817\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
-dimensional Ising model only has a critical point in
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 12820\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28723
. Thus
A
is certainly violated in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
. Furthermore in later numerical studies it was found that both
A
and
B
fail in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724

[
9
]
. It came as a surprise that numerical simulations in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725

[
10
,
11
]
gave instead strong indications for the both the emergence of supersymmetry
B
and the dimensional reduction
A
.
The Parisi-Sourlas conjecture can also be studied in a different random field model, RF
\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 28723}
, which has a phase transition between
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28722\mathchar 12604\mathchar 28728
. In this case numerical simulations indicate that the fixed point is always compatible with
A

[
12
]
.

While many studies were produced in the past decades, no fully conclusive explanation of when/why the conjecture works was found.
This motivated a revised study of the emergence of SUSY in RF models (arrow
A
) using a perturbative RG setup
[
13
,
14
,
15
,
16
]
.
This series of works showed in epsilon expansion that the PS CFT for the Ising case is reached in the vicinity of the upper critical dimension, namely at
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28726\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28943
for small
\mathchar 28943
. Conversely, when
\mathchar 28943
is of order
\mathchar 28721
, some SUSY breaking deformations develop a large negative anomalous dimension becoming relevant for
\mathchar 29028
less than a critical value
\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29027}
. A two-loop analysis predicts that
\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 12825\mathchar 28724\mathchar 314\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724\mathchar 314\mathchar 28727
, which is in perfect agreement with the numerical simulation described above. The same setup was also applied to the RF
\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 28723}
model, showing that in this case all SUSY breaking deformations remain irrelevant in all dimensions, again in agreement with the numerical results.
In summary it was found that the SUSY fixed point sometimes is not reached because of new relevant SUSY breaking perturbations. However, whenever it is reached, dimensional reduction is expected to always occur.
This thus provides an explanation for when/why the Parisi-Sourlas conjecture works.

While this could seem the conclusion of a story, by revisiting the topic new interesting directions emerged. In particular, as we mentioned, the arrow
C
of figure
1
was also studied in an axiomatic CFT context in
[
7
]
. In this paper it was first defined what is a PS CFT
d
. Then it was shown in which sense such a theory has a description in terms of a CFT
d-2
. It was shown that a huge part of the spectrum and OPE coefficients of (also non protected) superprimaries of the PS CFT
d
exactly matches the CFT data of the CFT
d-2
. E.g. the spectrum and OPE coefficients of
all
the scalar operators would match in the two theories (in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
). This result was obtained by using arguments only based on symmetry and therefore applies to any possible PS CFT
d
.
The work of
[
7
]
thus motivated a new question: given any CFT
d-2
is it possible to define a PS CFT
d
which dimensionally reduces to it?
This is what we refer to as the Parisi-Sourlas dimensional uplift, represented as the arrow
D
of figure
1
, and it is the focus of this paper.
In particular the results of this paper are in support of the conjecture that, independently on the properties of the CFT
d-2
, the uplift always exists.
This is mostly independent from random field models, meaning that the uplifted models may or may not emerge at the fixed point of a disordered system.
One may thus ask why should we study the uplift.
There are various motivations.

One important motivation is to understand the space of possible consistent CFTs, which is the ultimate goal of the conformal bootstrap.
If the uplift always exists, then it can also be iterated.
Therefore given a CFT
d
one could in principle define an infinite tower of PS CFT
d+2n
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots
as shown in figure
2
.

CFT
d
CFT
d+2
CFT
d+4
\dots

Figure 2
:

Iterated uplift.

There is an important catch: PS CFTs are non-unitary. Therefore figure
2
shows that the space of non-unitary CFTs is infinitely much larger than the one of unitary ones: for every unitary CFT there exists an infinite tower of non unitary CFTs in higher dimension. The tower in figure
2
has increasing amount of SUSY. It is interesting to speculate what could be the fixed point of this sequence (indeed this would live in infinite dimensions and would enjoy infinite SUSY).

Another very interesting motivation is to find the first examples of fully solvable non-trivial CFTs in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
. Indeed in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
there is no example of interacting CFT where the full CFT data is accessible exactly. The situation is much better in
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29028
where in the minimal models it is possible to compute spectrum, OPE coefficients and even correlation functions exactly.
The idea is thus to uplift all minimal models obtaining infinitely many towers of infinitely many solvable CFTs in all even dimensions.
Notice also that such models might be physically relevant. For example the uplift of the Yang-Lee minimal model describes the
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028
universality class of RF
\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 28723}
model (which also describes systems like branched polymers and lattice animals
[
12
]
). In this universality class there exists also a model defined by Brydges and Imbrie
[
17
]
(see also
[
18
]
) which has SUSY at the microscopic level. The microscopic SUSY ensures the model to dimensionally reduce all along the RG flow and not only at the IR fixed point.
In the same spirit, in
[
19
]
Cardy recently proposed a supersymmetric microscopic model that flows to the uplifted Ising model. In
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
this thus flows to the uplifted Ising minimal model. It would be very interesting to see whether new supersymmetric microscopic theories can be defined such that they flow to other minimal models.

Another motivation is to use the supersymmetry of the uplifted theory as a tool for solving problems of the original theory. This might sound surprising since we argued that the two theories are somewhat equivalent. However the information is packaged very differently in the two formulations and it can happen that the SUSY formulation is more suitable to answer some questions, as we shall exemplify in the following.

Other applications will appear as we will enter the details of the paper. For example the SUSY structure of the uplifted theory provides some powerful kinematical constraint on CFTs. In particular we will see that PS SUSY implies a set of
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28723
relations between conformal blocks in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
and in
\mathchar 29028
dimensions, which are interesting in their own rights. For example one of such equation was already obtained by Dolan and Osborn to compute conformal blocks in higher dimensions from the knowledge of conformal blocks in lower dimensions. With this work we find that such equation actually has a very clear physical interpretation in the SUSY theory.

The plan of the paper is as follows.
We start section
2
with a basic introduction on CFTs, which will be followed by a review on PS SUSY and dimensional reduction/uplift.

In section (
3
) we explicitly show how to extract different primary components from superspace correlators with two, three and four scalar insertions.
We show that all of them are (as expected) compatible with conformal symmetry which is a further check that PS CFTs are well defined.
In the four-point function case we show that there are 43 independent primary components in a generic four-point function which are obtained by acting with 43 differential operators (in the conformal cross ratios) on the lowest component.

In section
4
we show that these differential operators on
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
-dimensional conformal blocks can be written in terms of a linear combination of at most five
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional conformal blocks. We further analyze these relation and explain how the linear combinations are tuned to cancel possible poles of the conformal blocks.

In section
5
we provide some examples of how to use the supersymmetry of the uplifted theory as a tool to compute observables of the original theory.
We find that in the uplift of generalized free field theory (GFF) there exists an infinite class of non trivial correlators which have some vanishing primary components. In terms of four-point functions this means that some
of the differential operators of section
3
annihilate the correlator. We then show that this constraint can be used in two ways. Firstly to obtain a recurrence relation for the coefficients in the conformal block decomposition, which we also show how to solve in the case of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 86414091
in closed form for any
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037
(which is a new result in GFF). Secondly we explain how to use the differential operators to fix the correlator itself (up to some constants). Then we also show how this type of logic can be generalized to perturbative computations around GFF, giving rise to differential equations for the perturbative correlators.

In section
6
we start by briefly introducing the minimal models. We give an RG argument of why diagonal minimal models should have a well defined uplift. We then exemplify how to uplift all four-point functions of Virasoro primaries in the Ising minimal model. We then proceed at computing the first few terms of their conformal block decomposition. We end the section by commenting on the properties of the spectrum which must contain an infinite number of conserved higher spin currents.

Section
7
is devoted to a general property of all uplifted theories: they are expected to have a larger set of operators than the reduced ones. These extra operators are projected to zero under dimensional reduction. In section
7
we exemplify a class of such operators. We then consider the uplift of a
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 29028
GFF four-point function as a toy model to show how the larger kinematic space of the uplifted theory (in
\mathchar 28723\mathchar 29028
there are two independent cross ratios instead of the single cross ratio of one-dimensional CFTs) is exactly reconstructed by the extra operators of the uplifted theory, which in this case are the spin
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
traceless and symmetric operators.

In section
8
we conclude the paper and mention future directions. More details are deferred to the appendices. Finally, a Mathematica code with the definitions of the differential operators (and some extra checks) is attached to the publication.

2
Background and review of Parisi-Sourlas CFTs

CFT basics

\mathchar 29028

[

for a more detailed review).

Conformal blocks

2.3

In this section we present a short introduction to PS SUSY based on the more complete results of

.

In order to introduce PS supersymmetry it is convenient to work in a superspace
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}

\mathchar 29049^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 28939}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28946\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 28946}\}

\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 29028\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28946\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 28946}

A QFT with PS SUSY is invariant under superrotations and supertranslations which are the transformations that preserve the superspace distance

(2.21)

Every time an index takes the value
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28946\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 28946}
, then the operator changes grading. E.g.

It is also worth commenting on the R-symmetry of the model, which is
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
and it is generated by
\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 28946\mathchar 28946}
,
\mathchar 29005^{\bar{\mathchar 28946}\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
and
\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29005^{\bar{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 28946}
. It is easy to see that, given a scalar
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}

{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
has charge
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
under
\mathchar 29005^{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
, while
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28946}
has charge
\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
. The bosonic components are charged zero. Similarly by taking indices in the direction
\mathchar 28946\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 28946}
we select operators with different R-symmetry charge. E.g. given a vector
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}^{\mathchar 29025}
,
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 28946}
has charge
\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
,
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28946}^{\mathchar 28946}
has charge
\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722
, while
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\bar{\mathchar 28946}}^{\mathchar 28946}
has charge 0. As usual, R-symmetry is a global symmetry, thus all non-vanishing correlation functions must be invariant under
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
.

Since the theory has superconformal symmetry, it is convenient to classify the operators as superconformal primaries and descendants.
The super primaries are operators
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785
defined such that they are annihilated by the special superconformal generators,
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 24891{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
. Superdescendants are instead obtained by acting with derivatives
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29025}
on the superprimaries.

Correlation functions of local operators are restricted by superconformal symmetry. One-point functions take the form
\delimiter 69632778{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28942_{{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}}
, while two- and three-point functions are fixed as

(2.22)

\displaystyle\delimiter 69632778{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28723}\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785^{{{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723}\over\mathchar 28722}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28723}^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785^{{{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28722}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28723}^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785^{{{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\over\mathchar 28722}}}}}\,\mathchar 24891

2.4
Dimensional reduction

Any given PS CFT can be dimensionally reduced to a CFT in two less dimension
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12817\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
. Let us explain how this works.
The main idea is that, given a correlator in superspace, if one restricts all the insertions to

\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}_{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12817\{\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\hat{\mathchar 29048}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\}

, then one defines the dimensionally reduced theory.
Namely all reduced correlators are obtained as

Dimensional reduction: all points

It is well known that
any set of correlators which arises from any type of reduction automatically satisfies the axioms of crossing and conformal block decomposition as a usual CFT
{}_{{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}}
. What is special to this kind of reduction is that the resulting CFT
{}_{{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}}

3
3

3

The precise statement is that, whenever the PS CFT contains a conserved super stress tensor, then the reduced theory contains a conserved stress tensor (as long as
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28721
). When
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
, as expected, the reduced stress tensor does not exist as we shall discuss in section
7
.
and with a very small operator content, which is smaller (or equal) than the superprimary content of the original PS CFT
d

[
7
]
.
Both properties are very atypical since: 1) the stress tensor is not expected to be conserved on
\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}_{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}
since in principle the energy is allowed to leak orthogonally to
\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}_{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}
and 2) usually for every operator in a higher dimensional theory we expect infinitely many operators in the dimensionally reduced theory, and therefore a much larger spectrum.
These remarkable properties are due to a decoupling of infinitely many operators because of SUSY. Let us explain how this decoupling arises.

Restricting operator insertions to a hyperplane in the CFT literature is often called “trivial defect”. In our case the trivial defect breaks the
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
symmetry to
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
where
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
is understood as the conformal group in
{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}
dimensions and
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
is a global symmetry. The prescription to get dimensional reduction (
2.30
) focuses on the singlet sector of the
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
symmetry. For any given primary operator
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}
there is an infinite set of
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
singlets in the trivial defect. Indeed, in addition to the
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-singlet primary
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}\delimiter 84054785
considered in (
2.30
), one may also consider
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-singlet primary operators obtained as
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 12863}^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29038}{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 12863}
is the derivative in the direction perpendicular to the hyperplane
\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}_{{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}}
, namely
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 12863}^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29026}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29026}
. The statement is that all these operators decouple from the theory if they are inserted in correlators of only
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-singlets.
This can be easily understood, since the
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}
metric orthogonal to the plane has two free indices that cannot be contracted with anything to build an
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-singlet. Indeed

\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29026}\,\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 29033\,\mathchar 29026}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\qquad\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29026}\ \mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 29026\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\,\mathchar 24891

(2.31)

the former relation due to the orthogonality of the vectors
\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 29033}
and the perpendicular metric, while the latter due to the properties of the supertrace of the orthosymplectic metric which satisfies
\textrm{str}\,\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
.
A similar decoupling works at the level of operators with spin. In this case to define operators on the trivial defect we must project the tensor indices to directions either orthogonal or parallel to
\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}_{{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}}
. The only
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-singlets which do not decouple have all indices along the trivial defect
\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}_{{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}}
.
So in practice given a superfield
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 29017}
that transforms in a representation of
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
labelled by a Young tableau
\mathchar 29017
as in (
2.19
), the reduced field transforms in a representation of
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
labelled by the same Young tableau
\mathchar 29017
. It can happen that the dimension of the representation
\mathchar 29017
in
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
is zero. When this happens, the dimensionally reduced field vanishes, so the superfield
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 29017}
is projected to zero upon dimensional reduction. We will expand on this point in section

Because of this we can conclude that when a PS SUSY Lagrangian is available, not only dimensional reduction is available, but also the uplift.
Indeed the equivalence (

\hat{\mathchar 28958}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29025}\,\mathchar 314

(2.38)

This can be further generalized to any number or scalar fields and also to theories where the fundamental fields are not scalars, see for example the realizations for spinors and gauge fields presented in
[

]
.

2.6
Uplift of CFTs

While we showed that given a Lagrangian, its uplift is somewhat straightforward, this result does not help much in defining the uplift of CFTs which typically do not have useful Lagrangian descriptions.
However in CFT we can use the power of symmetry to uplift some observables for free.
This is indeed a basic idea in CFT where it is known that one can use conformal symmetry to set the operator insertions to special locations. E.g. by knowing two and three-point functions of scalar operators on a line one can recover their full dependence on coordinates, and similarly four-point functions of scalar operators are completely determined by their expression on a plane.
The same idea works for PS CFTs.
For these theories the dimensionally reduced two and three-point functions of scalar operators determine the uplifted ones as long as
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
, while for four-point functions of scalar operators the uplifted correlator is fully reconstruct if
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
.
The reconstruction in these cases is trivial and it amounts to replace

\hat{\mathchar 29048}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29049\,\mathchar 24891

(2.39)

in correlation functions. For example a two-point function
\delimiter 69632778\hat{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\hat{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69640972\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673}
is simply uplifted to
\delimiter 69632778{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673}
, where now the distance is computed using the
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}
metric instead of a
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}
metric.
Similarly for any scalar four-point function

\delimiter 69632778\hat{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\hat{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\hat{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 84054785\hat{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28724}\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 28724}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8705\hat{\mathchar 29048}_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
, as long as
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, then
its uplift is just given by
\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
where all distances are replaced by superspace distances.

Of course this prescription cannot be used to fix all possible observables. In general for any fixed dimension
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
, when the number of insertion is high enough and/or the operators have spin large enough, then a piece of the information of the correlation function cannot be reconstructed. One can easily see when this happens for any given observable by counting the number of cross ratios and invariant tensor structures
[
29
]
. E.g. in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
a four-point function is fixed in terms of a single cross ratio and therefore one cannot simply reconstruct a full PS CFT four-point function in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
, but only its restriction to a line (we expand on this point in section
7
).
This is also related to the fact that some operators of the PS CFT are projected to zero after dimensional reduction. This is easy to see, indeed in the PS CFT there can exist operators with an arbitrary number of graded antisymmetric indices
[
7
]
, but in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
dimensions one cannot antisymmetrize more that
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
indices otherwise the result is trivially zero. This will be further discussed in section
7
.

In this paper we will mostly focus on scalar correlators of
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28724
insertions in dimensions
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
so for these cases the uplift will trivially work.
It is interesting to stress that this is a huge number of observables which know a lot about the theory. Indeed we are saying that all possible four-point functions of scalar operators will be exactly uplifted. The latter contain in their conformal block decomposition a lot of information about operators with spin. It is an interesting open question whether the information contained in this infinite set of correlators is enough to fix the rest of the observables by e.g. applying bootstrap techniques. We leave this question for future investigations.

3
Extracting primary components of correlation functions in superspace

One of the results of this paper is to put on a firm footing the uplift of scalar
\mathchar 29038
-point functions for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28724
. For the uplift to make sense all components of the superspace correlator must behave appropriately as correlators of a CFT
d
. In the following we show how to obtain the different primary components from the superspace correlators for the cases of two, three and four-point functions. We explicitly show the form of all components and that they indeed have the correct covariance properties of correlators of primary operators.
Moreover we will show that all the primary components can be fully reconstructed by the knowledge of the lowest component, which is fixed by the dimensionally reduced theory.
This result will be important for the rest of the paper where we will input a four-point function of a CFT
{}_{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}
to obtain all the primary components of the uplifted four-point functions.
Since this philosophy works for any CFT
{}_{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}
(at least when
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28721
) this result also supports the conjecture that the dimensional uplift works for any theory.

in superspace, the correspondent primaries are defined as follows

We notice that the normalization of the two-point functions above may be zero. This happens when
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720

\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\over\mathchar 28722}}
and at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724\over\mathchar 28722}}
which is the dimension of a free field in the dimensionally reduced theory.
In section (
6

\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
will appear, in that circumstance we will explain how the vanishing norm (
3.13

(3.21)

As a comment, let us mention that it is a non-trivial consistency check that in all 14 cases these take the form of three-point functions of primary operators. The shifts
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
keep track of the correct scaling of the operators and thus the theory behaves correctly as a CFT
d
.
The OPE coefficients of the (primary) superdescendants are just related to the ones of the superprimary by some coefficients
\mathchar 29027_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
which are fixed by SUSY. Therefore by knowing the lowest component one can reconstruct all the other ones.

3.3
Four-point functions

Scalar four-point functions in superspace are written as (
2.24
), in terms of a single function
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
of superspace cross ratios
\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014
.
Of course since the theory is a CFT, all components of this superspace four-point function should be written in terms of usual
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional cross ratios
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972
. The lowest component is just
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
.
The other components are obtained by acting with a differential operator in
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972
that acts on
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
.
The latter can be computed by acting with some differential operators on a four-point function, as follows

{\bf\mathchar 28996}_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28946\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785

(3.22)

where
{\bf\mathchar 28996}_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}
and
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}
are defined in (
3.7
) and (
3.9
) while
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}
is a differential operator in
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972
which we computed for all 43 components. Some cases are too lengthy to be shown in a paper,

but they will be collected in a Mathematica file included with the publication.

(3.36)

The operator (
3.36
) has an explicit dependence on
\mathchar 29028
. This feature is shared with all the other operators which we are omitting from the text.

All differential operators are written in a form where the derivatives in
\mathchar 29045
and
\delimiter 69640972
are on the right and thus act directly on the function. This formulation is the most convenient for computations, however there may exist a different formulation which makes the differential operators more compact. We did not invest time in searching for compact rewritings, which we postpone for future work.

We checked that the differential operators automatically satisfy crossing. For example the crossing
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12836\mathchar 28723
is written as

\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785\big\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12836\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12836\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28955_{\vec{\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 560}}}{{\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84267779}\delimiter 69640972^{{{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723}\over\mathchar 28722}}}\mathchar 29045^{{{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28722}}}}}\,\mathchar 24891

(3.37)

where we assume that
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
is crossing covariant, namely
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69640972^{{{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723}\over\mathchar 28722}}}\mathchar 29045^{{{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28722}}}\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045\delimiter 84054785
(which must be the case since it comes from a well defined CFT
d-2
). Here we define
\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12836\mathchar 28723}
and
\mathchar 28955_{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8710\mathchar 28721
is the fermionic signature of
\vec{\mathchar 29011}
, namely it computes how many permutations of the fermionic operators are needed to restore the canonical order, e.g.
\mathchar 28955_{\bar{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28721{\bf\mathchar 28724}{\bf\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28955_{\bar{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28721{\bf\mathchar 28723}{\bf\mathchar 28724}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28955_{\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28722}{\bf\mathchar 28723}{\bf\mathchar 28724}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
.

We further checked the crossing
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12836\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12836\mathchar 28724
, which also work out correctly.
These equations generically map
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
into a different
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 560}}\delimiter 84267779}
, which is a check that all the differential operators are correct.
Moreover this implies that all primary components of the superspace four-point functions automatically satisfy crossing in all channels. This is an important check for the uplift, indeed we find that given one four-point function in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
we get 43 new four-point functions in
\mathchar 29028
dimensions which are crossing covariant and consistent with supersymmetry. In the next section we explain that they can also be automatically decomposed in
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional conformal blocks.

4
Conformal block expansion in PS CFTs

In this section we want to study how the different components of a four-point function are all compatible with the conformal block decomposition. This will give rise to
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28723
relations between conformal blocks in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 29028
dimensions.

As explained in section
2.3
, the scalar four-point function in superspace is determined in terms of a single function
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
of super cross ratios which can be decomposed in superspace superconformal blocks
\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
as in (
2.28
).
On the other hand
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
also defines the dimensionally reduced four-point function (which depends on the usual cross ratios
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972
) and thus it can be decomposed in
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-dimensional blocks

\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}\tilde{\mathchar 29025}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 314

(4.1)

where in principle
\tilde{\mathchar 29025}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}
and
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}
of the two decompositions could be different.
In
[
7
]
it was shown that they are actually the same
\tilde{\mathchar 29025}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}
, since also the conformal blocks are the same function (
2.29
).
This already explains that given a scalar four-point function in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
dimensions compatible with the conformal block decomposition (and other bootstrap axioms like crossing) one can define a scalar four-point function in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}
superspace which is compatible with the superconformal block decomposition (and the other bootstrap axioms).

However it is also important to check that the
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional PS CFT behaves as a good CFT
d
and therefore its four-point functions are decomposed in terms of
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional conformal blocks
\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
.
In particular this means that each component of
\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
should be decomposed in terms of a finite number of
\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
in a precise linear combination fixed by supersymmetry.
In
[
7
]
we showed that this is indeed true for the lowest component.
In particular since the lowest component of
\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
is also equal to the block
\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
itself,
we obtained the following beautiful formula relating conformal blocks in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 29028
dimensions
[
7
]

\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\,\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\,\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\,\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\,\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\ \mathchar 24891

(4.2)

where the generic scalar block in

The coefficients can be written in closed form as follows:

Because of this relation it is trivial to see that if
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
is decomposed in
\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}

\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
and the explicit form of the coefficients
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
can be used to simply map the OPE coefficients of the

\mathchar 29028
-dimensional one.

This relation however was obtained by only considering the lowest component of
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
.
Now we want to see how this relation generalizes for the other components.

4.1
43 relations between conformal blocks across dimensions

As we showed in section
3.3
, inside a single superspace four-point functions there are 43 inequivalent four-point functions specified by the list
{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}
. Using (
3.22
) we can reconstruct each primary four-point function by acting with a given differential operator
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}
in the variables
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972
on the function
\mathchar 29030\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
that specifies the lowest component. This is of course also true at the level of conformal blocks if we replace
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
in (
3.22
). In particular this replacement defines what are the superconformal blocks for each component. In other words
\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29013\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29014\delimiter 84054785
is the
superspace superconformal blocks
, while
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
defines what is usually called a
superconformal block
, which is just a function of
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972
. Each
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785
must be decomposed in a finite number of
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional blocks. Using again formula (
2.29
), we can replace
\mathchar 28999^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}
and thus obtain
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28722
extra relations between blocks in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 29028
dimensions labelled by the component
\vec{\mathchar 29011}
.
We can capture all such relations by the following compact formula

\boxed{\ \phantom{\Bigg\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29008_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}}\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\ \mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\,\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29033\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29034}\ \phantom{\Bigg\delimiter 69640972}}

(4.4)

where we recall that

\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
just implements some shifts in the conformal dimensions
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29035}
of the external operators by some units as defined in (

) and (

), while the differential operators
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}
are defined in section

We computed in a closed form all coefficients
\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
for all 43 possible choices of
\vec{\mathchar 29011}
as we will show in the following section and in appendix

(for convenience in the Mathematica file we also include the full list of the coefficients together with a check of (

)).

Finally it is crucial that the summation over
\mathchar 29008_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}
is finite; in particular it contains at most
\mathchar 28725

{{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}}
.
Indeed

4.2
Examples

In the previous section we introduced all ingredients for the equation (

) besides the coefficient
\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}

Let us first consider the case of
\vec{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 12349{\bf\mathchar 29033}
for
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 28724
, namely when there is a single level-2 superdescendant and all other operators are lowest components.
Let us exemplify in this case all the ingredients entering (
4.4
).
The differential operators
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84267779}
are defined in equations (
3.23
)-(
3.26
).
The charges
\mathchar 29009_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\mathchar 29009_{\mathchar 28722}
defined in (
4.6

\mathchar 29008_{\delimiter 67482370{{\bf\mathchar 29033}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}
which contains 5 terms.
We thus find that there are five
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional conformal blocks appearing in the
rhs
of (
4.4
), with
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}
shifted by two units (namely
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722
).
We therefore obtain that (

\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{{\bf\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28678_{{\bf\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28678_{{\bf\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{{\bf\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28678_{{\bf\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\,\mathchar 24891

(4.10)

where
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724
.

Let us mention a common feature that we will see for most of choices of
\vec{\mathchar 29011}
: the relations (
4.4
) can be though as equations for the conformal blocks, but the latter only depend on the differences
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}
and
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}
, while the coefficients
\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
(see e.g. (
4.11
)) as well as the differential operators
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
(see e.g. (
3.23
)) depend also on
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}
,
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}
(polynomially). Therefore one can expand the equations (
4.4
) in
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}
and
\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}
and each term of the expansion can be understood as a valid equation for the blocks.
