4.3
Poles in the conformal blocks and SUSY

In this section we aim at studying the possible singularities in
\mathchar 28673
of conformal blocks. Below we warm up by considering a unitary CFT and show that in this case the blocks are always finite even when they naively look singular.
Then we turn to a PS CFT and consider the superconformal blocks, which by equation (
4.4
) are written as a linear combination of conformal blocks. We will show that in this case it may also happen that some blocks in the linear combination diverge, but the specific linear combination provided by (
4.4
) is fine tuned to cancel all singularities and gives a finite result. On one hand this remark can be understood as a consistency check of (
4.4
). On the other hand it will also uncover some interesting features which will play an important role in the conformal block decompositions of the following sections.

To start let us consider the conformal block decomposition of a four-point correlation function in usual (compact unitary) CFTs. In this case it is expected that both the OPE coefficients and the conformal blocks appearing in the decomposition are finite quantities and sum up to a finite result.
The fact that conformal blocks are finite is non trivial since they have poles in
\mathchar 28673
as shown in (
2.12
), which for convenience we rewrite here

\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}\mathchar 12824{{\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}\over\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673^{\mathchar 8707}_{\mathchar 28993}}}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 352_{\mathchar 28993}}\,\mathchar 24891

(4.23)

where
\mathchar 28673^{\mathchar 8707}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28673^{\mathchar 8707}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352_{\mathchar 28993}
are defined in table
1
and the coefficient
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}
is defined in (
2.13
).
So the conformal blocks seem to diverge for the exchange of an operator with labels
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28673^{\mathchar 8707}_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352
.
At a first sight this might be puzzling since these labels include the ones of a free scalar (
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
) and a conserved tensors (with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
for any spin
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\dots
).
So naively it may seem that free scalars and conserved tensors cannot be exchanged.
How can this be compatible with the fact that these operators exist in physical theories? The answer is simple. In unitary theories when
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352
equals the ones of a free scalar or a conserved tensor then the associated residue
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}
vanishes to cancel the pole.
This gives a non trivial restriction on the possible set of correlation functions that exchange such operators.
For example for a free scalar exchange the conformal block diverges as

\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12824{{\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{III}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\over\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\mbox{for }\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314

(4.24)

The residue at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
vanishes when

\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{III}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349{{\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\right\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 28724\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720

(4.25)

which implies that either
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8710\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
or
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8710\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
. This is indeed what happens in free theory: in order to exchange a free field in an OPE, the dimensions of the (external) operators in the correlation function must differ by
\mathchar 8710\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
, e.g.
\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12824\mathchar 28958
.

Similarly when we exchange a conserved tensor of spin
\mathchar 352
the conformal block diverges as

\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 12824{{\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\over\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\mbox{for }\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 314

(4.26)

Thus we need to require that the residue at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
has to vanish, namely

\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 352\mathchar 24609\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 28722\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24609\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\,\mathchar 24891

(4.27)

which is possible only for either
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
or
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
. This condition is indeed required by Ward identities of the conserved tensors
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 29012^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12847\mathchar 28942_{\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}}
.

We thus found that in unitary theories the singularities of the conformal blocks are avoided because the residue
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}
is zero. Let us discuss what this means physically.
Let us consider a conformal multiplet/module labelled by a primary with dimension
\mathchar 28673
and spin
\mathchar 352
. Now we want to see what happens to this multiplet when changing
\mathchar 28673
.
Using the notation of equation (
4.23
), when
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28673^{\mathchar 8707}_{\mathchar 28993}
the module becomes singular/reducible, namely it contains a descendant labelled by
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352_{\mathchar 28993}
which becomes primary. The primary descendant together with all its descendants define the submodule.
All states of the submodule have vanishing norm. These are responsible for the pole in the conformal block.
The condition
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
is equivalent to the fact that there is a shortening condition that the primary descendant must satisfy.
For example
\square\mathchar 28958\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
for a free scalar or
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950}\mathchar 29002^{\mathchar 28950\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
for a spin
\mathchar 352
conserved current.
These shortening conditions can be understood as a way to mod out the primary descendant and its associated multiplet. This is necessary in a unitary theory to avoid non-trivial states with vanishing norms.
In the following we see how this does not happen in PS CFTs.

Avoiding poles with Parisi-Sourlas SUSY

Above we showed that a tuning of the conformal blocks is required when operators at the unitarity bound are exchanged. Here we exemplify how this is not necessary in PS CFTs. This will also generalize to exchanges below the unitarity bound which are naively singular (as we detail in appendix
B
).

Let us exemplify the new mechanism for the case of an exchanged scalar superblock with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
.
We further think of this superblock as arising from the uplift of a
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
-dimensional block. Since in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
dimensions nothing special happens for exchanges of operators of dimensions
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12349\hat{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722
, we would expect that the same is true for the superblocks of the uplifted theory, however this is non-trivial since in
\mathchar 29028
-dimensions the blocks have a pole at this value of
\mathchar 28673
(unless (
4.25
) is satisfied). Let us show how this pole is erased in the case of the lowest component of a four-point function.
The lowest component of a scalar (
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
) superblock takes the form

\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\mbox{where }\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704{{\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\right\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28673\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}}\,\mathchar 314

(4.28)

We can explicitly see that the coefficient
\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}
in front of
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}
diverges as follows

\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12824\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{III}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\over\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}}\,\qquad\mbox{for }\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314

(4.29)

The contribution of
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}
exactly cancels the pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
of
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}
defined in formula (
2.12
), and thus we get that the combination provided by (the lowest component of) the superblock

\tilde{\mathchar 29031}_{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12817\lim_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}

(4.30)

is finite.
Therefore in a PS CFT the pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
in the conformal block cancels thanks to supersymmetry and the conformal block
\tilde{\mathchar 29031}_{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}
can always be exchanged independently on the value of
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}
and
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}
.
This mechanism is unconventional because the pole of the block is cancelled because of a divergent OPE coefficient (to be precise it is only the kinematic part
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}
of the OPE which diverges). In all theories with finite OPE coefficients this can never happen and the only mechanism allowed is the one of the cancellation of the residues
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}
.

The fact that
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
means that the module of the primary descendant is not modded out and thus it will be part of the spectrum.
Another consequence of this is that we can consider these primary descendants as insertions in a correlation function without getting a vanishing result, because no shortening condition has to be satisfied (this is in contrast with the unitary cases described above where if one considers e.g.
\square\mathchar 28958
as an insertion in any free boson correlation function, the resulting correlator vanishes).
We will see how all these facts will play a role in the study of the uplifted minimal models.

There are other important cases where this mechanism takes place.
Indeed, besides for exchanges of operators at the unitarity bound, the conformal blocks diverge for infinitely more values of
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28673^{\mathchar 8707}_{\mathchar 28993}
(the unitary values appear only for the type
\textrm{III}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
when
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and for
\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
when
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
, but in general one could consider any of the three types
\textrm{I}\mathchar 24891\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\textrm{III}
for any
\mathchar 29038
). All these extra cases lye below the unitarity bounds, so these exchanges do not arise in unitary theories. However these may arise in non-unitary theories, like the PS CFTs. For such theories there should be a mechanism to cancel the singularities whenever one of these operators is exchanged.

First it is interesting to ask which are the possible problematic exchanges which arise in the Parisi-Sourlas uplift of a unitary
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12817\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
-dimensional theory (these are the theories that we consider in the rest of the paper).
The answer is easy: the operators of such uplifts satisfy bounds which arise by uplifting the unitarity-bounds of the lower dimensional theory.
Namely they satisfy
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12821{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
for scalar operators and
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723
for operators of spin
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
.
In particular in these theories one can exchange scalar operators with dimensions
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and operators with dimensions
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and spin
\mathchar 352
. For all these values the blocks have singularities.
For the uplift to make sense it is crucial that all these singularities are resolved.
In appendix
B
we show that all these poles are erased, sometimes because
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 28993}
vanishes and sometimes because of the special linear combination of the blocks provided by supersymmetry which gives rise to new regularized blocks that exactly cancels the singularity as we showed for (
4.30
).
The specific form of the regularized blocks computed in appendix
B
is going to play a role in the next sections, when we will decompose correlations functions of the uplifted minimal models.

5
Bootstrapping GFF using PS SUSY

In the previous sections we determined a set of kinematic properties shared by all PS CFTs.
We now want to apply the PS CFT framework to the simple theory of a generalized free boson field (GFF).
Below we will start by a basic introduction to GFF to set some conventions. We define it in
{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28721
dimensions (the case
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
is studied in section
7.2
) so that the uplifted theory lives in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28723
dimensions (for the purposes of this section one could also drop the hat of
{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}
in every formula). In the next subsection we will define the uplifted theory. We will show that, besides being well defined, the PS uplift of GFF can be used to infer some properties of the original GFF, thus helping to bootstrap some observables. As an example we will be able to compute, without doing any Wick contraction, the conformal block decomposition of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 86414091
for any power
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037
, which is a new result in GFF. We will also explain how the uplift can be used to constrain perturbative computations around GFF.

GFF can be defined through a non-local
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
-dimensional Lagrangian
\mathchar 28958\square^{\mathchar 28952}\mathchar 28958
, where
\mathchar 28952
is a real parameter. The theory is Gaussian and therefore is solvable. An equivalent way to define the theory without introducing a Lagrangian is as follows.
The defining property of GFF is that correlation function of fields
\mathchar 28958
can be computed through Wick contractions where the propagators are
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29048\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}}
. Since the theory enjoys a
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}
symmetry (which acts as
\mathchar 28958\mathchar 12833\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28958
) correlation functions of an odd number of
\mathchar 28958
fields vanish.

When
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}\mathchar 12349{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
(which corresponds to
\mathchar 28952\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
in the Lagrangian) the theory is the usual free theory, which has local equations of motions
\square\mathchar 28958\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and infinitely many conserved (higher spin) currents. However we shall study the more generic case of
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}
being a generic real parameter.
In this case the field
\mathchar 28958
does not satisfy any local shortening condition. Similarly no local composite of
\mathchar 28958
(built out of an integer number of
\mathchar 28958
dressed with an integer number of derivatives) can play the role of a conserved current/tensor, simply because it would not have integer conformal dimensions (which is required by representation theory). So naively we do not expect shortening conditions for any operator or in other words we do not expect the correlation functions of GFF to satisfy any local differential equation. In the next subsection we shall see that such differential equations can be defined because of the existence of the PS uplift.
Before that let us continue the review of GFF by describing some observables.

In GFF it is straightforward to compute correlation functions by Wick contractions. A simple example is

The same logic can be applied for any four-point function.
In the following we show how one can use the PS uplift as a tool to compute the conformal block decomposition of a four-point function of

In this case the conformal block expansion takes the form

Before explaining how this bootstrap problem works, let us present the simplest set of GFF correlators. Because of the tower of double twist operator, in a four-point function we typically expect the exchange of an infinite number of operators.
However there is a set of “extremal” four-point function where only one operator is exchanged. These take the simple form

5.1
Bootstrapping GFF using PS supersymmetry

Given the GFF in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
dimensions, we can obtain its relative PS CFT
d
by explicitly uplifting the Lagrangian of the theory,
namely
\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 28950}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28952}\mathchar 28958\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28952}\mathchar 28680
.
Like GFF, its uplifted version can be defined by saying that all correlators are computed by Wick contractions, where now the two-point function lives in superspace
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}}
, where
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}
is the same as in the dimensionally reduced theory.
When
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}\mathchar 12349{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
, the model becomes the uplift of free theory but we will keep
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}
generic.
9
9

9

For the connection of uplifted (generalized) free theory and random field models see
[
32
]
.

Composite operators are mapped by following the rules that
\mathchar 28958\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28680
and
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29025}
.

Now we show how the existence of the uplift can be used to constrain (the dimensionally reduced) GFF. For example to constrain GFF correlation functions, we can leverage the simple fact that

{\bf\mathchar 28996}_{\delimiter 67482370\bf\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\,\mathchar 24891

(5.7)

where
{\bf\mathchar 28996}_{\delimiter 67482370\bf\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12817\tilde{\mathchar 28680}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
is the superdescendant of
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 28720}
.
This two-point function vanishes simply because it involves two different primaries, namely
\delimiter 69632778\tilde{\mathchar 28680}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.

10

10

Notice that
\tilde{\mathchar 28680}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28961\mathchar 8235\square\mathchar 28967
. This primary is identically zero in the uplift of local free theory because
\square\mathchar 28967\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28961
and
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722
. On the contrary it is non vanishing in GFF, where we do not impose the latter constraints. In fact in GFF the operator
\tilde{\mathchar 28680}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
has a non vanishing two-point function.

Indeed any correlation function of the following form vanishes,

{\bf\mathchar 28996}_{\delimiter 67482370\bf\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\dots\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29035}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\,\mathchar 24891

(5.8)

for generic
\mathchar 29035
and
\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29035}
. This is true because the correlation function in GFF is computed by Wick contraction and therefore it is equal to as a sum of terms that contain a vanishing term
{\bf\mathchar 28996}_{\delimiter 67482370\bf\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28680\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.

5.2
Bootstrapping OPE coefficients

We now show how to use (

) and (

) to find recurrence relations for the OPE coefficients in the conformal block decomposition of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 86414091

) and act with the operators

In summary this method solves for the OPE coefficients that are harder to compute (the ones of double twist operators which contain derivatives), while the remaining ones can be obtained from a one-line combinatoric computation.
Moreover, at a more abstract level, PS SUSY provides a novel way to understand why double twist families (in GFF) are tied together: one cannot simply modify one of the OPE coefficients in the family without violating the recursion relations provided by supersymmetry.

5.3
Bootstrapping correlators and extension to perturbation theory

In the previous section we found a sophisticated way to extract information from supersymmetry.
There is also another simple way to make use these constraints. Indeed in (
5.10
) we found that a differential operator was annihilating the correlators, therefore we obtained a differential equation that the correlators must satisfy. Below we show how to use this information to fix the form of the GFF correlators
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 86414091
up to a few constants. Moreover we will see that this logic can be also used to bootstrap the form of correlation functions in perturbation theory around GFF.

Let us first see how to use (
5.10
) to fix the correlation function. We require that the GFF correlators are written as finite sums of powers
\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 29026}
. This is always true if the correlator contains a finite number of fields and derivatives. Then we notice that the differential operators
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
acting on
\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 29026}
produce a sum of terms with different powers, e.g.
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 29026}
is a linear combination of
\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 29026}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 29026}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 29026\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}
. Because of this reason and the fact that the correlator is written as a finite sum of powers, the action
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
must annihilate separately each power. By requiring

\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 29026}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\,\mathchar 24891

(5.23)

we obtain a constraint on the possible powers
\mathchar 29025
and
\mathchar 29026
.
If we apply this logic to
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 86414091
using e.g.
{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779
we find that there are only three allowed powers and thus the correlator must take the form

In this example we were able to obtain the ansatz (
5.24
) without doing any Wick contraction.
This may not seem a very impressive result since the direct computation of the four-point function is quite straightforward.
However this idea can be applied to more generic cases, where the computation is more involved as we show below.

The fact that
\mathchar 28996
-functions satisfy many relations is a well known fact in the literature. These can be obtained by manipulating the integrals (e.g. by taking derivatives in the propagators) and re-expressing the result as
\mathchar 28996
-functions with shifted
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}

It is however interesting that for us the relations (
5.32
) came without doing any computation. In practice it was sufficient to notice that the integral in
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28720}
cannot create more than one term
\tilde{\mathchar 28680}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
or one term
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 28680_{\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
, which automatically implies that the external operators have vanishing Wick contractions with the vertex.

Let us also mention that the relations (
5.32
) impose strong constraint on the shape of the correlator.
For example let us show what happens in the simplest case of
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
and
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
, where the form of the
\bar{\mathchar 28996}
function is known
[
23
]

\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12847{{\mathchar 29050\bar{\mathchar 29050}\over\mathchar 29050\mathchar 8704\bar{\mathchar 29050}}}\left\delimiter 67273472\log\left\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\bar{\mathchar 29050}\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29050}}\right\delimiter 84054785\log\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\bar{\mathchar 29050}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\text{Li}_{\mathchar 28722}\left\delimiter 67273472\bar{\mathchar 29050}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\text{Li}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 314

(5.34)

First it is easy to check that (
5.34
) is indeed annihilated by all the differential operators in (
5.32
).
Moreover we found that (
5.32
) can be used to bootstrap the correlator.
In particular one can consider the ansatz made by a generic linear combination of the three terms
{{\mathchar 29050\bar{\mathchar 29050}\over\mathchar 29050\mathchar 8704\bar{\mathchar 29050}}}\{\log\left\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\bar{\mathchar 29050}\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29050}}\right\delimiter 84054785\log\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\bar{\mathchar 29050}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\text{Li}_{\mathchar 28722}\left\delimiter 67273472\bar{\mathchar 29050}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\text{Li}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785\}
in (
5.34
).
17
17

17

This ansatz can be motivated by the degree of trascendentality of the functions appearing in a one-loop integral. Of course one could find a more restrictive ansatz or even the full solution
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}
by requiring that it satisfies extra conditions (e.g. we could impose that
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}
is invariant under
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12833\bar{\mathchar 29050}
and/or is a single-valued function of the cross ratios). Here we only wanted to show that (
5.32
) is also a powerful condition.
We noticed that requiring that the ansatz is annihilated by any of
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28723\bar{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 84267779}
,
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28724\bar{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84267779}
or
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 28722\bar{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 84267779}
(since
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28723\bar{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28724\bar{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370{\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 28722\bar{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
, these are just two independent differential equations) is sufficient to fix the relative coefficients of the three terms.

This section was a very basic sample of applications of the idea of bootstrapping correlators using PS supersymmetry, which should be understood more as a proof of concept. One could extend this to more sophisticated cases where the final answer is not known and leverage PS SUSY to obtain new results. We leave this direction for future investigations.

6
The
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028
Parisi-Sourlas uplift of diagonal minimal models

The minimal models are an infinite set of solvable two-dimensional CFTs. They can be completely fixed through bootstrap constraints which yield the exact spectrum of conformal dimensions and OPE coefficients. Their correlation functions can be also computed exactly giving rise to the best known examples of interacting CFTs. In the following we will start by a quick introduction to these models following
[
35
]
and then we will give an argument for why these should have a Parisi-Sourlas uplift to four dimensions. In the next subsection we will further investigate the uplift of the know correlation functions and show that they do not possess any inconsistency.

Minimal models are defined by requiring that their spectrum only contains a finite number of Virasoro primaries.
They are denoted as
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
and are labelled by two coprime integers
\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}
with
\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
which determine the central charge
[
36
]

\mathchar 29027\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28726{{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}}}\,\mathchar 314

(6.1)

The holomorphic weight of the Virasoro primaries is labelled by

\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349{{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29042\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}}}\,\mathchar 24891

(6.2)

with
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29040\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
. We will be mostly interested in the diagonal minimal models defined by
\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29040\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
, where all Virasoro primaries have identical holomorphic and antiholomorphic weights, namely they are labelled by
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\bar{\mathchar 29032}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043}
or equivalently
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29042\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.

Minimal models are known
[
37
]
(see also section 7.4.7 of
[
35
]
) to have an effective description in terms of the following Landau-Ginzburg Lagrangian,

\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4946\nolimits\mathchar 29028^{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 29048\ {{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 28958\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29014\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28958\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 24891

(6.3)

where
{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
.
By choosing a
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}
invariant polynomial potential
\mathchar 29014\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28958\delimiter 84054785
of degree
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29037
(
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\dots
) we obtain a multicritical system in the universality class defined by the diagonal minimal models
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
where
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
is Ising,
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
is the tricritical, etc.
Of course it is hard to use this action for actual computation since there is no small coupling. Typically one performs computation in
\mathchar 28943
-expansion (namely one works in
{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28943
dimensions where
\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29037\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
is the upper critical dimension) such that the interaction
\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29037}
is weakly relevant. One can then tune all the strongly relevant perturbation
\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29033}
with
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29037
and study the IR fixed point in perturbation theory for small
\mathchar 28943
. To recover the
\mathchar 28722
-dimensional results one must then set
\mathchar 28943\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
.
Similarly by introducing also
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}
-odd terms in the potential one can study a class of non-unitary minimal models, e.g. the cubic potential is in the same universality class as the Lee-Yang minimal model which corresponds to
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29005}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28725\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
.

The Lagrangian description (
6.3
) is not particularly useful to compute observables since minimal models can be solved exactly through bootstrap methods. On the other hand this formulation has a clear benefit for our purposes since it can be easily uplifted to
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722
dimension as we showed in section
2.5
. Indeed the action (
6.3
) can be trivially uplifted to

\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4946\nolimits\mathchar 29028^{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 29048\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28946\mathchar 29028\bar{\mathchar 28946}\ {{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 28680\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29014\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28680\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 314

(6.4)

One can consider this action in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
which reduces to (
6.3
) in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
, where both actions are strongly coupled.
18
18

18

Notice that when written in components the action becomes (
2.34
), where one can easily see that all polynomial interactions are relevant since
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28967\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.

Alternatively it is also possible to consider (
6.4
) in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28943
dimensions which corresponds to the uplift the
\mathchar 28943
-expansion of the model (
6.3
).
Since the superspace action (
6.4
) dimensionally reduces to the
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
action (
6.3
) for any
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
, then the
\mathchar 28943
-expansion of (
6.4
) matches the one of (
6.3
) for every
\mathchar 28943
. Therefore it is natural to assume that
\mathchar 28943
-expansion of (
6.4
) when continued to
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
, dimensionally reduce to minimal models.
The action (
6.4
) in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
therefore can be thought as a definition for the Parisi-Sourlas uplift of the diagonal minimal models.
While this Lagrangian description of the uplift is not particularly useful (as it was the case for the usual minimal models), at least it tells us that the uplift of the diagonal minimal models should exist.
This argument is also valid for the non-unitary minimal models which have a Lagrangian description (like Lee-Yang) and even for the Liouville model which is also defined through a scalar field action.
In the following we assume that indeed the Parisi-Sourlas uplift of the diagonal minimal models exists, and we see if any problem arises by looking at the concrete example of the Ising minimal model.

6.1
Example: Parisi-Sourlas uplift of the Ising minimal model

In this section we test the dimensional uplift on the explicit correlators of the
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29028
critical Ising model. This model contains the identity and two scalar Virasoro primaries called
\mathchar 28955
and
\mathchar 28943
, which respectively have conformal dimensions
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28728}}
and
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
. The model has a
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}
symmetry under which
\mathchar 28955
is odd while
\mathchar 28943
and the identity are even.

All correlators of Virasoro primaries can be computed in a closed form, see e.g.
[
38
]
. In particular the four-point functions in the notation (
2.4

\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28721\bar{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 28722\bar{\mathchar 28724}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29045\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29045\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\right\delimiter 84054785
. These results show that for any given four-point function it is straightforward to compute all its 43 components.
Of course many of the entries in tables
3
and
4
are redundant since they could be obtained by permutations of the external operators. However we decided to present all the components in order to show that they are compatible with crossing. E.g. the component
{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779}
and
{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 84267779}
are related by the crossing
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12836\mathchar 28723
which amounts to take the component
{\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779}
, change
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12836\delimiter 69640972
and multiply by
\mathchar 29045^{{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\right\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 69640972^{{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723}\right\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 69640972^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
.

We did repeat this exercise for the other correlators but we do not show the results here because the expression are lengthy, however we stress that this can be easily done in all cases using the differential operators
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
computed in section
3.3
.

Notice also that the expressions (
6.5
) determine all correlators of Virasoro primaries, but from these it is also possible to obtain the correlators global primaries by studying descendants under Virasoro. In particular we could build an infinite set of scalar global primaries by acting on each of these correlators with some appropriate choices of Virasoro generators (see appendix
C
). All four-point functions of scalar global primaries can be equally trivially uplifted to
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028
and for any such correlator it is straightforward to obtain the 43 components as above.

From this exercise we therefore conclude that we know a huge amount of information for the uplifted Ising minimal model. In this model we can in principle obtain all possible correlators of global scalar primaries and we can easily uplift any of them and obtain all its components as shown above. All components will automatically transform correctly under crossing, respect supersymmetry, and —because of equation (
4.4
)— they will also have a good four-dimensional conformal block decomposition. In what follows we will show some explicit conformal blocks decompositions of the uplifts of the Ising correlators.

6.2
Conformal block decomposition of the uplifted correlators

We now consider some four-point functions of the uplifted Ising minimal model and show how they decompose in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
conformal blocks. In principle this exercise is automatic since it is easy to compute the decomposition in two-dimensional conformal blocks (see appendix
C.1
) and for each such block one can use formula (
4.4
) to obtain the four-dimensional counterpart. However we find it useful to exemplify what happens in a few instances in order to discuss some features of the theory.
As we explained above, there are in principle infinitely many correlators of scalar primary operators which we could consider and for each of them we could further study 43 components. For the following examples we find it enough to only consider the uplift of all four-point functions of Virasoro primaries. Moreover we will restrict our attention to their lowest components, which in practice are exactly the ones in (
6.5
). In appendix
D
we further show the decomposition of a few higher components of the four-point function of
\mathchar 28943
.

\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}

Let us spell out some of the operators with interesting features.
The first operator is simply the identity with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and spin zero.

\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724

\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722

\mathchar 28722

\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722

We notice that the unitarity bound
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12821{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721

Many special operators appear in this decomposition.
Let us start by analyzing the spectrum of operators with even scaling dimensions. These correspond in
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29028
to the contributions of the Virasoro multiplet associated to the identity.
Like for the correlation function of
\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943
and
\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943
above we recognize the exchange of the identity, the super stress tensor
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29012}}^{\mathchar 29025\mathchar 29026}
and all possible conserved super currents
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 352}}
with even spin
\mathchar 352
.
In particular we consistently see the pattern that each
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
conserved tensors
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722
are always accompanied with a lower dimensional operators with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352
according to (
6.8
).

\mathchar 28943
.

which has the same quantum numbers of the
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29028

\mathchar 28943
. In
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724

\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
we would expect a singularity in the respective conformal block with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
(see e.g. formula (
4.24
) with
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}
).
However —as explained in section
4.3
— supersymmetry works by exactly subtracting the singularity. Meaning that the conformal block for the exchange of
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28720}
is divergent, but the singularity is exactly cancelled by the block of the exchange of the primary built out of
{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
, namely

\tilde{\mathchar 29031}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12817\lim_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28721}\left\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 28673^{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28726\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}}\,\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\right\delimiter 84267779\,\mathchar 24891

(6.14)

which, because of formula (
4.2
), is equal to its two-dimensional counterpart
\tilde{\mathchar 29031}^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29031^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}
.
To be precise, according to (
3.4
), the actual primary is defined as
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29048}^{\mathchar 28722}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673_{{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 320_{\bar{\mathchar 28946}}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28946}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 28946\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29048}^{\mathchar 28722}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}
, which is just the level-two descendant of
{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}
with dimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
and spin zero. This operator is both a primary and a descendant, and it has zero norm as it can be easily seen by considering the form of the two-point function (
3.13
) with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
. It is however crucial to notice that while being an operator with zero norm,
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
should not be modded out from the spectrum. Indeed even if the two-point function of
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
is zero, its higher point correlation functions with generic operators are typically non-vanishing. We can explicitly see this in the tables
3
and
4
, by considering any
\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779
that contain a bold entry.
E.g. the case
\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779
is just the four-point function
\delimiter 69632778\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 86414091
, which is clearly non vanishing. This can also be seen in full generality at the level of three-point functions by looking at the explicit formulae of section
3.2
, e.g. equation (
3.20
) with
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
.

In fact the presence of
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
is crucial since it plays the role of artificially shortening the multiplet of
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28720}
. By this we mean that the pole in the block of
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28720}
is associated to the exchange of the descendant
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29048}^{\mathchar 28722}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}
, but this contribution is cancelled by the exchange of the supersymmetric partner
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
. So we can conclude that the tilded block is in practice encoding the exchange of a shortened multiplet of
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28720}
where one removes the submultiplet of
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29048}^{\mathchar 28722}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}
because of a tuned addition of the multiplet of
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
.

As an observation, we find it interesting that in two-dimensions the field
\mathchar 28943
can be considered as a product of chiral free fermions
\mathchar 28943\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28960\bar{\mathchar 28960}
. On the other hand in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
the field
\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}_{\mathchar 28720}
has the dimension of a free boson. In both cases this operator looks like a free field in a strongly coupled theory. However the reason why this happens in the two situations is somewhat different. In
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
the reasoning is that the operator
\mathchar 28955
does not have a simple local expression in terms of
\mathchar 28960
, so correlators involving
\mathchar 28955
cannot be obtained by Wick theorem. On the other hand in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
the operator
{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}
only shares the quantum numbers of a free field, but it does not satisfy the same equations of motion, namely
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29048}^{\mathchar 28722}{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
, where
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
is a good primary operator in the spectrum.

(6.17)

where
