is the complete elliptic integral of the first kind.

It is interesting that the operators in (
6.15
) come from the uplift of Virasoro descendants of
\mathchar 28943
in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722

(e.g.
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 28943
has dimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29035
and spin
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29035
). On the other hand
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}_{\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}
in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
have the same quantum numbers of generalized currents, namely they satisfy
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723
. Operators with these quantum numbers typically satisfy a conservation equation of second order in the derivative, however in this case the conservation does not hold namely
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}}{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}_{\mathchar 28720}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
(as in the case of
{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}
the shortening condition is artificial and due to the exchange of
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
).
So we see that, as
\mathchar 28943
is uplifted to an operator with the same quantum number of a free field, the uplift of the Virasoro descendant of
\mathchar 28943
also have the flavour of an infinite set of (generalized) currents which one expects to find in free theories. However in both the cases the uplifted operators do not satisfy the shortening conditions which one expects in free theory and therefore are fundamentally different.

In the conformal block decomposition (
6.12
) there are also other operator exchanges in the same supermultiplet of
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 352}}
, e.g.
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}_{\mathchar 28720}
which has dimensions
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
and spin
\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
. These other operators lye above the unitarity bounds and are associated to non-singular conformal blocks.

6.3
Comments on the operator spectrum

Let us summarize some features of the spectrum of the uplifted Parisi-Sourlas minimal models.
First let us review some features of the original two dimensional models.
In
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
, given a Virasoro primary
\mathchar 29007
, one can build all possible Virasoro descendants applying the Virasoro generators
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 29038}
as follows
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\bar{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}^{\bar{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29007
. The resulting operators have quantum numbers
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29007}
and
\bar{\mathchar 29032}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\bar{\mathchar 29035}}\bar{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29037}\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8235\bar{\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29007}
(in the case of the two dimensional Ising model
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28955\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28943
and thus we get three towers of operators with respectively
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032_{\mathchar 29007}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29032}_{\mathchar 29007}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28726}}\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28726}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785
).
If the module is non-degenerate all the Virasoro descendants are independent, however in minimal models all modules are singular, namely there are relations between Virasoro descendants —the BPZ equations
[
36
]
— which should be modded out.
Between the independent Virasoro states one can generate all the global primaries by requiring that they are annihilated by
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28721}
.
It is easy to see that the number of the global primaries exponentially grows with the Virasoro level.
In appendix
C
we show the construction of such primaries and their counting more explicitly in the Ising case.

Every global primary in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
has an uplifted superprimary counterpart which itself has various components. We could see the presence of such operators directly in the conformal block decompositions above.
We noticed that any holomorphic or antiholomorphic global primary in the identity module (namely any operator built out of
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28721
such that
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 28721}
annihilates it and similarly for the antiholomorphic case, as shown in appendix
C
) gives rise to a superfield
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 352}}
with superdimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352
. The number of such supercurrents grows exponentially in
\mathchar 352
(see appendix
C
).
The supermultiplet of each
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 352}}
in contains various interesting primaries which we explicitly observe in the conformal block decompositions.
21
21

21

It is worth noticing that the Virasoro identity multiplet can be exchanged only in the OPE of equal operators, which is consistent with the fact that the higher spin currents can also only appear in the OPE of equal operators.

The primaries
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}_{\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}}_{\mathchar 28720}
play the role of four-dimensional conserved currents of respectively spin
\mathchar 352
and
\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
. These appeared in the decompositions above. Moreover there are also other important components of the same supermultiplet, like the fermionic currents
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28720}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}_{\mathchar 28946}
which appear in the decomposition in appendix
D
. They have dimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
and spin respectively equal to
\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 352
. These operators satisfy a generalized conservation equation of the type
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}}\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}}\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}_{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.
Finally there exists also a component
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28946}
which has charge two under
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
, and satisfies
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722
with spin
\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
. This also satisfies a conservation equation
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}}\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
.
With each such current one can in principle construct topological operators that in turn define conserved charges (whose number will also grow exponentially in
\mathchar 352
).
This property is shared by all minimal models since it only relies on the existence of the Virasoro multiplet of the identity.

Let us also stress that in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
all the different global currents discussed above can be packaged in a unique Virasoro multiplet. This structure should play a role also in the uplifted theory. However in the uplifted theory for every current one can build more charges than in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
. The simplest example is that of (super)translations. Indeed in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
we can build a topological operator by integrating the stress tensor to define the translations
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
and
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
.
When we do the same using the super stress tensor in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
we can build six different types of supertranslations: four bosonic and two fermionic.
Only two of the bosonic ones are related to
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
and
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
, while the other four are new charges that cannot be defined in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
.
If we believe that these charges will be packaged with all the ones of the higher spin tensors inside a single algebra, we should imagine that the resulting algebra must contain infinitely many more charges than Virasoro. It would be very interesting to better understand this infinite-dimensional algebra in the uplifted theory.

It is also important to mention that the existence of higher spin conserved currents in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
is in contrast with the no-go theorem of
[
39
]
, which showed that under some assumptions the only theory with higher spin symmetry in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
is free theory.
However this is not a paradox since one of the assumptions of
[
39
]
is unitarity. Therefore
[
39
]
does not apply to the PS uplift of the minimal models.

The rest of the operator content depends on the chosen minimal model. It happens that the case of the Ising model is somewhat special since it also contains the operator
\mathchar 28943
which has dimension one. Because of this fact we find that in the supermultiplet of its uplifted counterpart
{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}
there exists the operator
{\mathcal{\mathchar 28997}}_{\mathchar 28720}
which has the same quantum numbers of a free scalar but does not satisfy equation of motions. Similarly in the same Virasoro multiplet of
\mathchar 28943
there are all the (anti) holomorphic global primaries which again they are defined by
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 28943
(and barred version of it) which are annihilated by
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 28721}
(
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28721}
) as in appendix
C
. The uplift of the latter give rise to the superfields
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 352}}
with superdimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
(again the number of such superfields grows exponentially with
\mathchar 352
). In their supermultiplet there exist the primaries
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}_{\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}
which have
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723
. Operators with these quantum numbers typically satisfy the generalized conservation equations
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}}{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}_{\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
, but in this case this equation does not hold so these currents are not conserved. A similar story works for other components of
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}
, like
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}_{\mathchar 28946}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}
and
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29017}}_{\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}
which have the quantum numbers of higher spin conserved currents but they do not satisfy shortening conditions as one can infer from (
B.7
) and (
B.8
).
The fact that all these operators do not satisfy shortening conditions implies that the theory contains zero norm states which are not modded out.
This would in principle be a problem in the OPE, since the exchange of null states would give rise to poles. However because of supersymmetry other operators in the same supermultiplet are perfectly tuned to cancel the poles.
This is an instance of the general discussion of section
4.3
.

Finally for the case of the Ising model one can study the uplift of the Virasoro multiplet of the spin operator
\mathchar 28955
. Since the latter has rational dimensions it does not give rise to shortened global representations. However we can point out that the uplifted field
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}}
has lowest component
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}}_{\mathchar 28720}
with dimension
{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28728}}
which lyes below the unitarity bound and similarly that the uplift of all the holomorphic global primaries in the multiplet of
\mathchar 28955
have lowest component with dimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28728}}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352
which lye below the unitarity bound.

To complete the discussion on the spectrum we should mention that in the uplifted theory there should exist new operators which do not exist in the dimensionally reduced theories. It would be interesting to see if it is possible to reconstruct them by performing a bootstrap analysis of correlators that cannot be trivially reconstructed from their lower dimensional counterparts, e.g. by considering the four-point functions of spinning operators (e.g. stress tensors) or by increasing the number of insertions (e.g. the six-point function of
\mathchar 28955
or
\mathchar 28943
in the Ising model). This might be possible since we know a huge amount of exact information for the model.
Another possible strategy to fill out this missing information is to better understand the underlying infinite dimensional symmetry of the uplifted theory and see if this can be used to completely solve the model, in the same spirit as it was done for its two-dimensional counterparts.

7
Comments on the loss of information in the reduction

In this paper we always considered cases when the uplift works trivially.
In particular we could simply use the prescription (
2.39
), which replace the insertions in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}
with insertions in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722}
.
This is possible when the symmetry of the observable is sufficient to reconstruct the full kinematic dependence of the
\mathchar 28722\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722
extra dimensions.
However in some cases this complete reconstruction cannot be done. Here we want to mention a couple of explicit examples.

7.1
On the operators that dimensionally reduce to zero

In Parisi-Sourlas theories one is allowed to define some operators in
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
representations which cannot possibly be reduced to non vanishing operators in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
dimensions.
The representations of
\mathchar 29007\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29040\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
allow for superprimaries labelled by a Young tableau of
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779
rows and one arbitrarily long column as in (
2.19
) (where rows are graded symmetrized and columns graded antisymmetrized).
In particular one can consider a superprimary with an arbitrary number of graded antisymmetric indices.
This for sure cannot have any dimensionally reduced counterpart since there are no available representations of
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
labelled by these Young tableau.
Namely it is not possible to antisymmetrize an arbitrary number of indices of a
\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-tensor and therefore such operators are projected to zero in the dimensionally reduced theory.
In order to make this less abstract we want to give a very explicit construction of such class of PS CFT operators which projects to zero.

A simple way to study this problem is to consider a Parisi-Sourlas
\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
vector model defined by the action,

\mathchar 4946\nolimits\mathchar 29028^{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 29048\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28946\mathchar 29028\bar{\mathchar 28946}\ {{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29014\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 24891

(7.1)

where the summation of the vector index
\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038
is understood and
\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28967_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\bar{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28960}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28946\bar{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29033}
.
This model can be reduced to

\mathchar 4946\nolimits\mathchar 29028^{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 29048\ {{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 28950}\hat{\mathchar 28958}_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29014\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 28958}_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 314

(7.2)

For simplicity we shall work in the free case
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
, but similar considerations would also apply in perturbation theory.

As we explained in section

, the map between the superprimaries of the PS CFT and the primaries of the reduced theory is given by replacing

\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12833\hat{\mathchar 28958}_{\mathchar 29033}

\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28939}

\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12833\hat{\mathchar 28958}_{\mathchar 29033}\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\mathchar 28680^{\mathchar 28722}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12833\hat{\mathchar 28958}_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\mathchar 28680_{\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 29025}\mathchar 28680_{\mathchar 29034\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28958_{\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 320^{\mathchar 28939}\hat{\mathchar 28958}_{\mathchar 29034\delimiter 84267779}\,\mathchar 314

(7.3)

These are different operators, the first is a scalar

-vector, the second is a scalar
\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785

We could continue writing infinitely many of such examples which have a

Let us however consider the superprimary

23

23

For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\delimiter 67482370\hat{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779

\delimiter 67482370\hat{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\hat{\mathchar 29028}

\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12349\hat{\mathchar 29028}

\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28939_{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}}\hat{\mathchar 29007}^{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28939_{\hat{\mathchar 29028}}}

Conversely for every
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12606\hat{\mathchar 29028}

\hat{\mathchar 29007}^{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}
vanishes because it is not possible to antisymmetrize
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721

\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
possible values.
In the latter case we thus conclude that the superprimary
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}
is projected to zero in the dimensionally reduced theory. This means that it will not be part of the spectrum of the reduced theory and thus that when we uplift the reduced theory we will not be able to get any information about such operator.

To conclude, (
7.4
) gives a very explicit example of a non-trivial operator that always dimensionally reduces to zero independently on the chosen dimension
\mathchar 29028
(when
\mathchar 29038
is large enough).
While this was a fairly specific construction,
we expect that the uplifted theory will generically contain a larger spectrum with respect to the reduced one, where the extra operators project to zero when dimensionally reduced.
Of course any correlation function which contains at least one of these operators will dimensionally reduce to zero. A less trivial statement is that
these operators strongly affect the dimensional reduction also when they can be exchange in the OPE.
In the following we give a precise example of this phenomenon in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
.

7.2
On the uplift of one-dimensional GFF

In this paper we studied the cases when the prescription
5.1
fully reconstructs the uplifted observables, however sometimes this is insufficient. The simplest example is when we uplift a
\hat{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
four-point function to
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
. This procedure cannot reconstruct the full
\mathchar 28723\mathchar 29028
correlator because the latter is a functions of two cross ratios while in
\mathchar 28721\hat{\mathchar 29028}
only a single independent cross ratio is available. Let us show in more details what happens in this case. In particular we focus on the case of GFF, where we know both the
\mathchar 28721\hat{\mathchar 29028}

\mathchar 28723\mathchar 29028

It is easy to define the PS uplift of
\mathchar 28721\hat{\mathchar 29028}
GFF. Indeed following the definition of section

\mathchar 28721\hat{\mathchar 29028}

In the next we shall focus on

\mathchar 28993\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\left\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 29050\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29050}}\right\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}}\mathchar 8235{\mathchar 29050}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}}\mathchar 314

Let us now discuss how to possibly uplift this result to
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
.
If we are only given the final expression for the correlator (without knowing to which uplifted theory it corresponds), our only way to define the uplifted correlator becomes the replacement (

Indeed

\mathchar 28944^{\mathchar 28722}

So in practice (

Now that we explained that the complication in the uplift is due to the different operator content of the two theories, we may further ask whether starting from (
7.15
) one could reconstruct (
7.7
).
We did not attempt it here but we think that this might be possible.
In particular we notice that (
7.15
) contains the correct spectrum and OPE coefficients of the part of the spectrum which is not projected to zero, namely all the scalar operators.
Moreover, if on one hand (
7.15
) misses completely the information of all spinning superprimaries, we never imposed the constraint of crossing symmetry and we expect that the expression (
7.15
) is not crossing invariant (one may try to prove this since the blocks at
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
are known exactly in every dimension, but we did not attempt it).
It would be interesting to see whether by requiring crossing symmetry and possibly inputting extra information about the uplifted theory one could actually reconstruct the uplifted correlator from the one on the line. We leave this problem for the future.

As a final comment it is interesting to notice that the uplift (
7.7
) can be understood as a better analytic continuation of GFF to one dimension. In the sense that by continuing the OPE and spectrum as function of the spacetime dimension we would find (
7.7
) and not (
7.11
). Also the uplift (
7.7
) is expected to have all properties of a CFT
3
, which has a richer structure (e.g. the operators should lie in Regge trajectories which are analytic in spin) and can possess a conserved stress tensor (when
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28958}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722
). It may be interesting to investigate better the uplift of one dimensional theories to give them a higher dimensional interpretation. This study could be also fruitful in order to find new ways to export tools which are better understood in one dimension (e.g. the analytic functionals of
[
41
,
42
,
43
]
) to higher dimensions.

8
Conclusion and outlook

In this paper we started the study of the Parisi-Sourlas uplift of CFTs.
We explained that it is possible to uplift correlators of a generic CFT
d-2
to define correlators of a PS CFT
d
.
25
25

25

As reviewed in the introduction, PS CFTs are known to have physical applications for models with random fields, e.g. they describe the RF Ising model in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725
and the RF
\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 28723}
in
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12604\mathchar 28728
.

In particular any given correlation function in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
dimensions gives rise to a superspace correlator in
\mathchar 29028
dimensions, which can then be expanded in components generating a set of correlators of primary operators in the uplifted theory. We explicitly checked that for two, three and four insertions of scalar operators the kinematic structure of all components is (as expected) compatible with conformal symmetry.
All components can be extracted by the action of known differential operators in superspace. In section
3.3
we further show that four-point functions have 43 independent components which can be obtained by applying 43 differential operators
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
in
\mathchar 29045
and
\delimiter 69640972
on the lowest component of the four-point function.

In section
4
we provide a very robust check that for any CFT
d-2
its uplifted PS CFT
d
makes sense.
Indeed we check that for any scalar four-point function in the CFT
d-2
, the 43 components of the uplifted correlator have a good
\mathchar 29028
-dimensional conformal block decomposition.
This is ensured by formula (
4.4
), which rewrites the action of
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
on block in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
dimension as a linear combination of at most five conformal blocks in
\mathchar 29028
dimensions. Formula (
4.4
) is also a beautiful result for the theory of conformal blocks, indeed it provides very interesting relations between blocks shifted by two dimensions.
26
26

26

Notice also that relations between conformal blocks can be used to find relations for exchange Witten diagrams
[
44
]
.

Between such relations we find (
4.22
) which was already obtained by Dolan and Osborn in
[
30
]
to map lower dimensional blocks to higher dimensional ones. Our work gives a clear physical interpretation of such equation.

PS CFTs are non unitary CFTs and by dimensional uplift we expect that their conformal block decomposition often contains exchanges of subtle conformal block which naively seem to diverge. However in section
4.3
, by analyzing formula (
4.4
), we showed that all these singularities are cancelled. Physically the singularities of the blocks signal the presence of null states in the spectrum of the theory. In unitary theories these are cancelled by modding out the null states. In PS CFT we show that null states can also be part of the spectrum without giving rise to singularities in correlators, because these are subtracted by other conformal blocks contributions with singular OPE coefficients. This structure is ensured kinematically at the conformal block level thanks to PS SUSY.

These results can also be understood as new checks that both PS CFT make sense and that they can arise from the uplift of any CFT
d-2
.
While some checks were also performed in
[
7
]
, in that paper it was given most attention to the lowest component of the superspace correlation functions, which is the one entering the dimensional reduction. In this paper we wanted to give extra motivation for the uplift, and therefore also the other components were carefully studied.

We then give specific applications of the uplift. In particular we focused on models that must possess a well defined uplift (because of Lagrangian arguments). The first case is GFF, while the second one consists in all the diagonal minimal models.

In section
5
we defined the uplifted GFF explaining how to compute any observable by using Wick contractions in superspace and we show that this description is sometimes very useful as a tool. In particular some observables of the original GFF are constrained by the supersymmetry of the uplift. The constraints arise in GFF because of factorization (therefore our computations can be extended to any theory with this property), namely the fact that any GFF correlator can be written in terms of product of two-point functions. In the uplift we find that some components of some infinite families of correlation functions vanish. This happens because their factorized form always contains a two-point function of different primaries (belonging to the same supermultiplet), which therefore is zero.
In practice this reasoning tells us that some classes of GFF correlators are annihilated by some differential operators. In the case of four-point functions these differential operators are a subset of the 43 possible
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
defined in section
3.3
. Combining the fact that
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
annihilates the correlator and that it maps a block in lower dimensions to a sum of blocks in higher dimensions according to (
4.4
), we find that PS SUSY gives rise to recursion relations on the OPE coefficients of the double twist exchanges. We showed this for any four-point function of the form
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 86414091
for any power
\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29033}
.
For all such four-point functions we find seven recurrence relations, which can be compactly written as in (
5.16
). In the case of
\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28723}
we show that these relations can be easily solved by (
5.21
). This is a new result on GFF which was obtained by only assuming the existence of double twist operators and imposing the supersymmetry of the uplift. It is quite remarkable that such minimal assumptions fix the form of infinitely many OPE coefficients of infinitely many correlators.

On the philosophical side, the PS SUSY of the uplift defines a new mechanism (besides the usual one of analyticity in spin) which links together different operators of the same double twist family.
Indeed the recurrence relations (
5.16
) just descend from the fact that a given spin
\mathchar 352
double twist superprimary contains in its supermultiplet a number of primaries of different spin and dimensions. So around GFF, it happens that primaries belonging to different double twist supermultiplets have the same quantum numbers and recombine in such a way to give zero OPE coefficients in the conformal block decomposition, which in turns give rise to vanishing correlation functions. In practice the fact that some components have correlation functions requires a miraculous tuning of all the infinitely many OPE coefficients in the double twist family.

Besides solving for the OPE coefficients, we can also use the fact that
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
annihilates some correlators to solve for the correlators themselves. We showed this in some simple cases. In particular we could fix the correlator of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28958\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 28958^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 86414091
up to three constants without doing any Wick contraction.
Moreover we show that this same logic can have application at perturbative level (around GFF). For example we explain that the D-functions (which also describes contact Witten diagrams in AdS) are also annihilated by some of the operators
\mathchar 28996_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
and that this can be helpful to bootstrap their form. This idea can be surely extended to a large set of diagrammatic computations giving rise to a new tool to bootstrap correlators in perturbation theory.
This would be very interesting to explore. On a similar note it would be interesting to check whether the uplifted formulation has benefits for the analytic conformal bootstrap, which is also perturbative in nature.

In section
6
we turned our attention to the uplift of diagonal minimal models. We gave a simple RG argument which explains why the uplift of such models should exist. The idea is that diagonal minimal models can be obtained as the fixed point of the (multicritical) RG flow of a scalar Lagrangian. Since scalar Lagrangians can be uplifted, then these RG flows can be uplifted and so it must be the case for their IR fixed points. We focused on the Ising model and considered the four-point functions of Virasoro primary operators (namely combinations of
\mathchar 28943
and
\mathchar 28955
). We explained that they can be trivially uplifted to four dimensions (as it is the case for all scalar four-point functions) and showed as an example the 43 components of the uplift of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\delimiter 86414091
. We then proceeded to the decomposition of the uplifted four-point functions in
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028
conformal blocks. This analysis shows explicitly that in the spectrum of the uplifted minimal models there exist infinitely many conserved higher spin currents. These were expected because they also exist in the minimal models themselves. Such operators can be in principle used to define an infinite set of conserved charges which extends the one of the minimal models.
It would be very exciting to find the explicit expression for the charge algebra, which certainly deserves further investigations.

Interestingly the integrability properties of minimal models (meaning the existence of infinitely many commuting charges) should uplift to higher dimensions, giving rise to
examples of non-trivial higher dimensional integrable models (these would be truly integrable in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
without resorting to an auxiliary integrable
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29028
description). Notice that the existence of infinitely many conserved currents in a strongly coupled theory is in contradiction with the no-go theorem of
[
39
]
, however for PS CFTs this no-go theorem does not apply because it relies on unitarity.

As a curiosity, the uplifted Ising model has special features with respect to other uplifted minimal models. This is due to the Virasoro multiplet of
\mathchar 28943
. By uplifting the operators in this multiplet we find that they map to
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028
operator with the same quantum numbers of free fields and conserved currents. However we show that, despite the resemblance, the uplifted fields do not satisfy shortening conditions. Namely the
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028
multiplets contain null states which are not modded out from the spectrum of the theory. In order to prove that the exchange such multiplets is well defined we make use of the observations of section
4.3
which allows us to conclude that the conformal block decomposition of the uplift of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\delimiter 86414091
does not give rise to singularities even if zero norm states are exchanged.

As we mentioned in the introduction, some of the uplifted minimal models have a microscopic definition in terms of a supersymmetric statistical physics model
[
17
,
18
,
19
]
. It would be interesting to see whether this type of construction can be extended to other uplifted minimal models.

All along the paper we focused on observables that are “equivalent” in the CFT
d-2
and the PS CFT
d
, meaning that they can be dimensionally reduced or uplifted without ever loosing information (or in other words the missing information of the uplift can be fully recovered using the symmetry of the model).
From this, it might seem that the uplifted PS CFT
d
is just equivalent to a CFT
d-2
, but this is actually incorrect. The PS CFT
d
contains the whole information of the CFT
d-2
but it also contains more operators and OPE coefficients, so it is a bigger theory. Indeed it is easy to see that some operators of the PS CFT
d
are dimensionally reduced to zero. We exemplify a class of such operators in section
7
. Moreover in the same section we also explain that the projection to zero of these operators is responsible for the smaller kinematic space (e.g. the number of cross ratios and tensor structures) in some dimensionally reduced correlators. Indeed it is well known that the kinematic space of correlators depends on the spacetime dimensions and it might seem surprising that the uplift should make sense also when the two kinematic spaces are different.
The easiest example is that scalar four-point functions in a CFT
1
depend on a single cross ratio, while their uplifted PS CFT
3
counterparts depend on two.
In section
7
we give the example of
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 29028
GFF and its
\mathchar 28723\mathchar 29028
PS uplift. The PS GFF contains spinning operators which are absent in
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 29028
and we show that these are responsible for creating the dependence on the extra cross ratio in four-point functions. We believe that this picture should hold in general, e.g. also in relation to the number of tensor structures in correlators of spinning operators. It would be very interesting to check if this is indeed correct.

The existence of operators that dimensionally reduce to zero gives rise to a technical complication in the uplift of theories, like the minimal models, that are defined only in terms of a CFT (and not with a Lagrangian like GFF). Indeed for such theories we do not have access to a part of the spectrum of the uplifted models and one may ask whether it is possible to reconstruct it only by CFT considerations. While we do not have an explicit answer to this question we think that by imposing the knowledge of the reduced theory along with crossing symmetry it might possible to reconstruct the missing information. It would be very interesting to show if this is the case.
Indeed we think that the following conjecture might be true:
27
27

27

This conjecture probably requires a lower bound in the dimensions, which might be
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
.

given any CFT
d-2
, its Parisi-Sourlas uplift exists and is unique.

Let us comment on this conjecture. As far as the existence, all results of this paper and of
[
7
]
point to the fact that the uplift automatically holds just by kinematics, without ever requiring specific properties of the CFT
d-2
. So this suggests that all CFTs should have an uplift.
About the uniqueness, we find it very unlikely to have two different PS CFT
d
realizations that share such enormous part of their spectrum (which indeed defines a full non-trivial CFT
d-2
) and yet differ on the CFT data of some more exotic spin representations. E.g. in
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
all the four-point functions of scalar superprimaries of the uplifted theory are completely fixed by the CFT
d-2
input. We think that the associated CFT data is too large to admit different completions. It would be very interesting to find a proof of such statements, establishing rigorously the existence and uniqueness of the uplift of generic CFTs.

A very interesting extension of this paper is to consider correlators of operators with spin. This has various applications. First one can extend the relations (
4.4
) to spinning conformal blocks. Also it would be interesting to see how the spinning setup constrains the CFT data of theories which factorize. It would be nice to see whether one can reconstruct some spinning correlators of the uplifted minimal models, like the ones involving the stress tensor. This is not a straightforward uplift since the
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29028
spinning correlators contain more tensor structures with respect to their
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29028
counterparts. However we might hope that such correlators will be constrained enough by superconformal Ward identities.

A very promising idea is to use the uplift of spinning correlators to simplify the numerical conformal bootstrap of setups that involve spinning operators like conserved currents and stress tensors (these setups were sometimes considered in the literature
[
45
,
46
,
47
]
, but they are very heavy both to implement and at the level of the numerics).
Let us explain how this new strategy works. The main idea is to use the basic fact that the supermultiplet of spinning operators (e.g. of spin one and two) contains scalar operators. One could thus perform a bootstrap study of such scalar operators in the uplifted theory to learn about the constraints of the spinning operators in the original theory. Notice that, while the uplifted theory is non-unitary, the unknown OPE coefficients are actually the lower dimensional ones, which satisfy the usual positivity properties that allow for efficient implementations of the numerical bootstrap. We do not know how much information would be encoded in this uplifted scalar bootstrap, but it is surely worth exploring this direction.

Finally one can also study deformations of the PS CFTs. Indeed we might hope that in the near future some PS CFTs like the uplifted minimal models will be fully solved. So it is natural to ask whether one can define new theories by deforming the uplifted ones. If the deformations preserve PS SUSY, then the flows can be dimensionally reduced and thus they would again correspond to uplift of known theories. However one could also deform the model by operators that break (part of) the supersymmetry and get completely new higher dimensional models which do not have any lower dimensional counterpart. This could be a new tool to shed light on the space of higher dimensional QFTs.

Appendix B
Resolving the singularities of the conformal blocks

As we explained in section
4.3
, there are various conformal block exchanges which naively diverge in the PS uplift of a unitary CFT.
These correspond to scalars with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and operators with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and spin
\mathchar 352
.
In this appendix we show that all the these exchanges are well defined, and non-singular.

\boxed{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}

First we pursue the study of the exchange of an operator with the same labels of a free scalar in a PS CFT. In section
4.3
we explained what happens to the lowest component of the four-point function but we need to investigate also all other
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28722
components. The result is that for all
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28726
components that belong to the set
\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}
(like the lowest component) the same argument of section
4.3
holds.
In particular we checked that all components
{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}
of the scalar superblocks collapse to the sum of two terms

\tilde{\mathchar 29031}^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12817\lim_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}

(B.1)

where we checked that the coefficients satisfy

\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12824\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{III}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}\over\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}}\,\mathchar 24891\qquad\mbox{for }\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314

(B.2)

So, as above, for all these cases, supersymmetry implies that the block
\tilde{\mathchar 29031}^{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}
is finite. For the
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28726
cases belonging to
{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}

the situation is easier because the superblocks reduces to a single scalar block with dimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722
, which has no poles. For the remaining case in
\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}
the scalar superblock collapses to zero, so again no singularity is present.

\boxed{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}

We now consider the case of the exchange of an PS superblock with spin
\mathchar 352
and dimension
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
. Again we can think of this as arising from the uplift of a
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
-dimensional block with the same labels. As before in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
dimensions there are no poles for this exchange so the same should happen also in
\mathchar 29028
, even if this is not trivial since a single conformal block has a pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
.
In this case the superblocks of all 26 components
\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}
generically contain five contributions of
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}
, where
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
is the only one that has pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
. The combination

\tilde{\mathchar 29031}^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 12817\lim_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370{\vec{\mathchar 29011}}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\,\mathchar 24891

(B.3)

Now we turn to the uplift of blocks that are at the unitarity bound in

\boxed{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}

Let us start by a the exchange of a scalar block with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722

For simplicity we focus on the case

\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8710\left\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\right\delimiter 84054785\,\mathchar 314

(B.10)

Let us consider the components
\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}

\lim_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\,\mathchar 24891

(B.11)

where the remaining single block diverges as
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12824{{\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{\tiny III}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8704\delimiter 67273472{{\mathchar 29028\over\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28724\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}

Indeed after imposing (

•

•

or

In both cases the resulting superblock is non-singular.
This resolves all singularities for the components
\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}
. The situation for

where

\boxed{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724}

Finally we turn to the exchange of a conserved tensor in
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
dimensions, namely an operator of spin

and dimension

\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\,\mathchar 314

(B.13)

Let us start by analyzing the components
\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}
of the superblock and check that the five conformal block contributions are non-singular.
First we find that two blocks never contribute because, when (

\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
. Therefore the superblock collapses to

\lim_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 352}\,\mathchar 314

(B.14)

The three blocks naively have a pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724
, but we shall argue that these singularities are always avoided.
First it is useful to notice that for all
\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}
the action
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
on (
B.13
) is such that
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722
. Keeping this in mind it is easy to see that the block
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 352}
has no pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724
because the residue
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723}
vanishes.
The fate of the remaining two contributions depends on whether
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
acts trivially or not.
When
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
the blocks
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 352}
and
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}
have no singularity at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724
because the residue
\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}
at this pole vanishes thanks to (
B.13
).
Conversely for all
\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779
such that
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}
is non-trivial, we checked that the coefficients
\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}
are always zero which implies that the associated blocks are not exchanged (this is important since the residue
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}
at their pole
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724
can now be non-vanishing because of the shift).

Let us now consider the components in
\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}
(which all satisfy
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8710\mathchar 28721
).
First we checked that all
\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
vanish because of (
B.13
). This leaves us with a superblock

\lim_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29027^{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352}\,\mathchar 24891

(B.15)

which is finite. Indeed, while in principle the two blocks have a pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724
, the associated residue
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722}
vanishes when
\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28678_{\delimiter 67482370\vec{\mathchar 29011}\delimiter 84267779}\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8710\mathchar 28721
.
Finally in the case of
\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785}
the block
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
has a pole at
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 8235\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28724
but the residue
\mathchar 29010_{\textrm{II}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}

(
B.13
).

With this we finished the study of possible singularities of the Parisi-Sourlas uplift of all
\hat{\mathchar 29028}
-dimensional blocks with
\mathchar 28673
above/at the unitarity bounds. We conclude that, because of supersymmetry, the uplifted blocks are such that the singularities are always cancelled.

Appendix C
Global multiplets inside Virasoro multiplets

In this appendix we study the global primaries inside Virasoro multiplets.
Given a Virasoro primary
\mathchar 29007
we can build the possible states in the module as
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\bar{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}^{\bar{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29007
. Global primaries are annihilated by
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28721}
, while descendants are created by applying
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
on global primaries.
In order to count the holomorphic global primaries we can take the holomorphic Virasoro character
\mathchar 28959_{\mathchar 29032}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 29032}\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 561}{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 29035}}}
and multiply it by
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84054785
, indeed
{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041}}
counts the descendants in a global multiplet. So the number of global primaries in a non-singular Virasoro multiplet is encoded by
\mathchar 28959_{\mathchar 29032}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28959_{\bar{\mathchar 29032}}\delimiter 67273472\bar{\mathchar 29041}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\bar{\mathchar 29041}\delimiter 84054785
.

We are interested in the multiplets which appear in minimal models. These are by definition singular, and thus satisfy BPZ shortening conditions.
Let us first focus on the identity multiple which satisfies the condition
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}
for all
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
(and similarly for the antiholomorphic part). The possible states in the module are defined by
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\bar{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}^{\bar{\mathchar 29038}_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}
, which can be global primary or descendants. E.g.
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}
is a global primary, while
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}
is a descendant.
Let us focus on just the holomorphic part of the multiplet, which is very important since it only contains global conserved currents of spin
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28673
, where
\mathchar 352
also counts the Virasoro level (see
[
48
]
for a related discussion).

10

The constants
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 29033}
in table
10
parametrize the dimensionality of the space of such operators at each level. E.g. at level eight there are three constants
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 28723}
and thus three possible independent primaries.
It is easy to generate a table of all such primaries at given level (e.g. with a simple code we could generate the 192 primaries at level 30), but this explicit computation quickly becomes too expensive since their number grows very fast.
In order to count the number
\mathchar 29040^{\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 29038}
of such holomorphic primaries at level
\mathchar 29038
one can use the following character,

\mathchar 28959^{\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\textrm{ global}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 561}{{\mathchar 28721\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785}}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 561}\mathchar 29040^{\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 29038}\,\mathchar 314

(C.1)

This is built as the generating function of all states in the multiplet
\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 561}{{\mathchar 28721\over\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785}}
where the multiplication by
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84054785
removes all the global descendants. We subtract one and add one in order to eliminate from the counting all the descendants of the identity since they vanish.
It is easy to see that
\mathchar 29040^{\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28942_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721}
, where
\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29038}
counts the number of partitions of the integer
\mathchar 29038
.

.

Using the known large
\mathchar 29038
behaviour of the partition of integers, it is straightforward to find that asymptotically
\mathchar 29040^{\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 29038}
grows as

\mathchar 29040^{\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785}_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12824{{\mathchar 28953^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29029^{\mathchar 28953\sqrt{{{\mathchar 28722\over\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 29038}}\over\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28724\sqrt{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 29038^{\mathchar 28722}}}\,\qquad\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

(C.2)

This means that in the Virasoro multiplet of the identity there is an exponentially growing number of higher spin currents of increasingly large spin (recall that
\mathchar 352\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29038
).
Using the global conserved current it is possible to construct charges, which will satisfy an algebra inherited from the Virasoro one. It is worth mentioning that there exists an infinite dimensional abelian subalgebra which can be constructed by considering a special linear combination of these currents for every even spin (see e.g.
[
48
]
).
Finally in order to get all the global primaries inside the Virasoro multiplet, one only needs to combine the holomorphic and antiholomorphic modules (e.g.
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathbb{\mathchar 29001}
is a scalar global primary with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724
). The resulting operators however are typically not holomorphic (antiholomorphic) and they do not define conserved currents.

A similar construction works for the multiplets of
\mathchar 28943
and
\mathchar 28955
. For these cases the shortening conditions are
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28943\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 28723\over\mathchar 28724}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28943\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28955\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 28724\over\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28955\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
(and similarly for the antiholomorphic part). One can generate again the global primaries in the multiplet by requiring this shortening condition and that they are annihilated by
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28721}
. In table
12
we present the explicit form of the first few operators.

C.1
Global conformal block decomposition of minimal model correlators

Let us perform the conformal block decomposition of the four-point functions of Virasoro primaries (
6.5
) in the Ising model.
We present in table
14
the decomposition of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\delimiter 86414091
, in table

the one of of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\delimiter 86414091
, in

the one of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28955\delimiter 86414091
, and finally in

the one of
\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\delimiter 86414091
. The notation is as usual that
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29007_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29007_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29007_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 29007_{\mathchar 28724}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}\,\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28673\,\mathchar 352}
(where we suppress the dependence of the external operators in

Table 17
:

Decomposition of
\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955\mathchar 28955
. The table on the top contains even dimensions exchanges which belong to the identity multiplet with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720
. The table on the bottom
contains odd dimensions exchanges which belong to the
\mathchar 28943
multiplet with
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28721
.

It is important to notice that all the decompositions are compatible with tables
11
and
13
. In particular the latter predict when a coefficient
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352}
is missing.
On the other hand the opposite is not true, meaning that there are more operators in tables
11
and
13
then the ones that are really exchanged in the OPE.
This might be puzzling at first, but it is ultimately due to the infinitely many symmetries that minimal models possess. Such symmetries impose new selection rules which at first sight are not expected, e.g. they must ensure that the coefficient
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28673\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352}
with
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28723\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28728\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724\delimiter 84054785
in table
16
vanishes. We checked that this is indeed true because the OPE coefficients is zero. This can be shown by using the precise form of the level four global primary defined in table
12
and applying it to the three-point function with
\mathchar 28955
and
\mathchar 28943
.
Similarly there is an extra selection rule in the OPE of equal operators which forbids the exchanges of global primaries built with an odd holomorphic and/or antiholomorphic level. So for example
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723}\bar{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 28943
is not exchanged in
17
.

there are 43 correlators in

which we can in principle decompose in conformal blocks.

So far we only considered components in
\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785}

\mathchar 29008^{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}

In table
22
and
23
we show respectively the decomposition of the component
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28721{\bar{\mathchar 28724}}\delimiter 84267779
and
\delimiter 67482370{\bf\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28722{\bar{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84267779
of
\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943\mathchar 28943

This is generically expected since we are taking the OPE of two different operators (even with different bosonic/fermionic statistics).
A more detailed property of these decompositions is the presence of new operator exchanges inside the supermultiplet of the supercurrents
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 352}}
. In particular we see superblock exchanges of the type (
B.15
) which are made of two finite contributions (because
\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28673_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28724}
). The two finite exchanges correspond to the operators
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28720}
and
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}_{\mathchar 28946}
(or better their primary counterpart which we call
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28720}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}_{\mathchar 28946}
) which have dimensions
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
spin respectively equal to
\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 352
. These operators satisfy a generalized conservation equation of the type
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}}\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 320_{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28722}}\tilde{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352}}_{\mathchar 28946}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
. From these operators it is therefore possible to build conserved charges.

First we notice that there are exchanges of novel components of the supercurrents, namely
{\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}}^{\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28950_{\mathchar 352\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28946}_{\mathchar 28946}
, which satisfy
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 352\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722
and thus have the quantum numbers of usual conserved currents in four dimensions.
We also notice that because of the form of (
4.22
), to each block in lower dimension we have at most one block in higher dimension. Moreover (
4.22
) is such that all scalar exchanges are automatically projected to zero (and similarly
\mathchar 28673\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
exchanges). Because of these reasons the decomposition is extremely sparse and contains even less terms than the original one in table
14
.
It is quite interesting that the action of the differential operator (
3.34
) on a correlator provides a new crossing covariant correlator where all scalar contributions are subtracted.
It would be nice to find a bootstrap application of this observation.
